- **Alternatives considered:** Building the schedule kind now (no customer names the mode — inventing contract ahead of the validation answer, however well-evidenced [7], is the pattern these decisions exist to prevent); diurnal as a `sequencer` table (phase drift — the mechanism and the requirement disagree about time itself); a wall-clock read inside the kind (violates decision 4 outright); deferral with no revisit condition (a silently dropped specified mode — the recorded condition keeps `WW-CTL-003`'s acceptance honest about what it does not cover).
- **Consequences:** `WW-CTL-003`'s acceptance excludes diurnal profiling until the revisit condition trips, and the requirement entry records the exclusion. The deferral costs nothing structurally — the `dose` port keeps the composition point open and the tick-mapped-time-input pattern needs no change to decision 4 — while the specified mode stays on the record rather than disappearing.
- **Affected tickets:** #240 (this record); the M10 schedule-kind ticket is filed only if the revisit condition trips; requirement `WW-CTL-003`; relies on #134, #196 and decisions 4, 45, 50; evidence `docs/research/chemical-dosing.md` (#232) source [7 §3.5].

### 55. Dosing skid alarm set

- **Status:** Accepted — the lifecycle is implemented; the set's mapping is proposed. Implemented basis: `latching-alarm` (#109) carries the Float two-flag semantics and `bool-latching-alarm` (#219) the Bool sibling — `alarm` the standing condition, `unacknowledged` latched on a fresh assertion until the model-wired writable `ack` point reads true, acks journaled through the ordinary settled receipt, the latch carried in checkpoints (decisions 33, 43); `alarm-monitor` stays the auto-clearing sibling (#22); the alarm pane consumes `alarm`/`unacknowledged` status-role ports (#108); decision 38 holds shelving, suppression, and classes deferred as per-kind extensions.
- **Problem:** The owner-specified skid lists fix the dosing alarm set — pump fault and no-discharge, tank low and empty, loss of the pacing signal, bund flood, external inhibit, dose not confirmed [7, 9, 12–16] — and each must map onto the decision-38/43 two-flag lifecycle as a wired `latching-alarm` or `bool-latching-alarm` instance without reopening the lifecycle, including the note's open question of whether a clamped demand alarms or only flags.
- **Chosen approach (proposed):** The set, each element named to its seam:
  - *Pump fault / no discharge* — `motor`'s computed `fault` plus the pump's own fault and no-discharge field signals [8, 13, 16], each into a `bool-latching-alarm`.
  - *Tank low* and *tank empty* — the two-stage level evidence distinguishes the warn-at-low from the stop-at-empty condition [12, 13]: a Float tank level feeds `latching-alarm` instances at declared limits, or field Bool switch points feed `bool-latching-alarm` instances; the empty condition also feeds the permissive chain's trip wiring (decision 51).
  - *Loss of pacing signal* — the ratio kind's `fallback_active` output (decision 50) into `bool-latching-alarm`; the vendor evidence treats signal loss as a fault-relay condition [13, 15].
  - *Bund flood* and *external inhibit* (wet weather) — field `Bool` points into `bool-latching-alarm` instances [7, 9], simultaneously feeding the permissive trips; whether bund, cabinet, and environmental alarms live in the dosing contract or the station's is the note's recorded assumption.
  - *Dose not confirmed* — `deviation-monitor`'s `deviating` output (decision 53) into `bool-latching-alarm` where a measured-consumption signal exists; absent one, no automated condition exists — the recorded limit.
  - *Clamped demand* — `clamped` is a `Status`-role flag on the faceplate and an ordinary trendable point; whether it additionally alarms is the note's open assumption, and the wiring supports either answer — a `bool-latching-alarm` on the flag if the customer requires the alarm.
  Which alarms latch until acknowledged versus auto-clear is per-alarm model wiring — `latching-alarm`/`bool-latching-alarm` versus `alarm-monitor` — and the required latch set is the note's recorded customer-validation question, assumption-marked.
- **Alternatives considered:** A dosing-specific alarm kind or alarm vocabulary (no new semantics — every element is a standing condition plus a latch the existing two kinds already express, and a skid-specific kind would fork the two-flag language the pane, history, and checkpoints speak); alarming every condition including `clamped` by default (invents alarm load the owner specs do not state — the note records the question as open, so the flag stands and the alarm stays a wiring choice); folding the skid alarms into `flow-paced-ratio` as computed outputs (the conditions' sources are distributed — field points, the motor, the deviation check — and alarm composition is model wiring per decision 43's pattern, not kind internals).
- **Consequences:** The skid alarm set needs no contract work beyond the decision-50/53 outputs it consumes: every alarm speaks the two-flag language the pane, history, checkpoints, and journal already carry, and acknowledgment needs no new command path. The open assumptions — clamp-alarm-versus-flag, the latch set, alarm ownership between the dosing and station contracts — stay flagged for customer validation.
- **Affected tickets:** #240 (this record); the M10 dosing-composition fixture ticket wires the alarm set (to be filed); the scripted acceptance-slice ticket asserts each raised alarm (to be filed); requirements `WW-CTL-003` and `WW-OPS-002`; relies on #109, #219, #22, #108 and decisions 33, 38, 43; evidence `docs/research/chemical-dosing.md` (#232) sources [7, 9, 12–16].

### 56. Backwash-supply arbitration — the bank coordinator

- **Status:** Accepted — the contract below is proposed; the M10 implementation tickets (to be filed) land it. Implemented basis it builds on: `pump-group` (#210, decision 41) is the group-kind precedent — indexed per-member ports discovered from bound names under the `trip_N`/`in_N` convention (`interlock` #38, `bool-gate` #152), group status outputs, descriptor roles, and checkpointed group state — though its stage-all-demanded semantics are the opposite of mutual exclusion; `bool-gate`/`sr-latch`/`edge-trigger` compose Bool conditions and edges (#38, #152); writable internal `In` points carry operator values through the receipted, actor-attributed command path (#30, #49, #165, decisions 14, 18, 39); `dcs-build`'s `PlantBuilder` composes multi-kind plants with the per-kind spec table pinned by the spec-drift guard (#112, #151, decision 31); and the reference station's builder-level assembly is the composition precedent (#220).
- **Problem:** The note's gap 3 — the central contract question. The washwater supply serves one filter at a time at the declared rate — the structural norm across the evidence [1 §6.3.a.12, 3, 5, 6, 14] — so contending backwash requests need an exclusive grant, an ordered queue with observable position [8, 9], grant permissives on the shared resources (washwater supply [9, 14], waste capacity [5, 9], the online-flow disturbance bound [8]), and release on completion or abort. Nothing existing expresses this: `pump-group` stages demanded members rather than serializing them; a FIFO-plus-reorder queue has no `bool-gate`/`sr-latch` expression; a `dcs-build` composition could emit the wiring but holds no per-scan run state and no descriptor surface. The queued filter's meanwhile state — keep filtering until granted versus offline with standby cover — varies by owner [8, 9] and must be declared, and operator queue reorder [9] needs a receipted surface where the contract exposes it.
- **Chosen approach (proposed):** A new `backwash-coordinator` kind in `dcs-blocks` plus a `dcs-build` builder-level bank-composition helper — the decision-41 split applied again: arbitration is per-scan stateful policy (an ordered queue and the held grant are run state), so it belongs in an executor component, while the N-filter bank as one typed artifact — one coordinator, N sequences, the shared-supply equipment — is construction-time composition on decision 31's seam, resolving the note's gap 10 alongside gap 3.
  Declared I/O (proposed; per-filter ports indexed `_1`…`_N` under the established convention, the filter count discovered from bound port names): per filter `i`, `request_i` (`In`, `Bool` — the armed backwash request from decision 57's sequence), `grant_i` (`Out`, `Bool` — the exclusive supply grant, at most one asserted at a time), and `position_i` (`Out`, `Int` — the 1-based queue position, `0` while not queued; what the granted filter reports is the implementing ticket's documented convention); bank-level `active` (`Out`, `Int` — which filter holds the grant, `0` while none does), `queued` (`Out`, `Int` — the pending count), and `resource_blocked` (`Out`, `Bool` — a request stands first in queue while a grant permissive fails) — the status outputs decision 61's alarm set and the note's gap-9 journaled bank events consume.
  Grant permissives are declared inputs — `supply_ok`, `waste_ok`, `flow_ok` (`In`, `Bool`: washwater supply available [9, 14], downstream waste capacity [5, 9], the online-flow disturbance bound [8]) — each aggregated upstream through `bool-gate`/`interlock` wiring, because which conditions feed each permissive is plant-specific data, not kind semantics — the `avail_i` division decision 41 records. A non-`Good` permissive reads as not-OK, the fail-safe reading the library already uses. The grant asserts only while every declared permissive holds, so a request can stand armed and first in queue — `resource_blocked` — without the wash starting.
  Parameters (proposed — `Int` codes where `Value` has no enum, the decision-41 convention): `queue_policy` (`Int` — `0` FIFO, the evidenced default [8, 9]; `1` priority-by-trigger, plausible but not evidenced; `2` operator-managed) and `queued_state` (`Int` — `0` keep filtering until granted; `1` offline with standby cover [8, 9]). Both are the note's recorded customer-validation assumptions carried as declared data rather than defaults; `queued_state`'s effect — which equipment actions "offline" and "standby cover" mean — composes in per-plant wiring from `request_i`/`grant_i` (decision 57), while the parameter makes the choice checkable contract data.
  Operator reorder rides the receipted command path: a writable internal `In` point bound to a declared reorder input carries the queue-order change as a receipted, actor-attributed `WriteValue` — the precise encoding is the implementing ticket's documented choice. Whether the first client requires reorder authority at all is the note's recorded assumption; a plant that does not expose it leaves the point unbound.
  Grant lifecycle: the grant holds while the granted filter's `request_i` stands and releases the scan it drops — completion and abort release identically through the sequence's request drop (decision 57), so the arbiter needs no completion vocabulary of its own.
  Checkpoint obligations: the queue contents and order and the held grant are run state captured and restored under decision 20's rule — a promotion mid-queue must not drop or reorder requests (the note's proposal; sources do not address controller redundancy — assumption-marked).
- **Alternatives considered:** A documented wiring convention from `bool-gate`/`sr-latch` (rejected per the note — the queue is ordered run state no Bool fold holds, and a convention gives every bank its own unverifiable arbitration); `dcs-build` builder-level composition emitting the arbitration as model data (rejected — construction-time wiring cannot hold a queue, decision 41's identical reasoning for `pump-group`); mutual exclusion through `interlock` trips per filter (expresses "not both" for a pair but no N-way order, queue position, or permissive-conditioned grant); arbitration left to operator discipline (the grant permissives and one-at-a-time norm are engineered constraints the evidence treats as control behavior, not procedure [3, 8, 9]).
- **Consequences:** One kind carries the exclusive-grant contract and every bank behavior names its seam — permissive aggregation stays declared wiring, reorder rides the existing command path, and the status outputs feed the alarm and journal surfaces with no new contract there. The assumption-marked choices — queue policy, reorder authority, the meanwhile state, the grant-permissive set — stay flagged for customer validation rather than silently defaulting. The `Int`-coded vocabulary is recorded honestly per decisions 41 and 50; the kind ships its `dcs-build` spec under the drift guard, and its checkpoint vocabulary deploys to both peers under decision 11's strictness.
- **Affected tickets:** #253 (this record); the M10 `backwash-coordinator` kind ticket — the kind plus its `dcs-build` spec under the drift guard (to be filed); the M10 filter-bank composition ticket — the builder-level bank helper and the checked-in fixture (to be filed); the scripted acceptance-slice ticket (to be filed); requirement `WW-CTL-004`; relies on #210, #38, #152, #30, #49, #165, #112, #151 and decisions 11, 14, 16, 18, 20, 31, 39, 41; evidence `docs/research/filter-backwash.md` (#233) sources [1, 3, 5, 6, 8, 9, 14].

### 57. The backwash step contract — measured advance and per-step equipment pattern

- **Status:** Accepted — a proposed contract on implemented seams; the M10 tickets (to be filed) land it. Implemented basis: `sequencer` walks a declared tick-timed step table reporting `step`/`done` under `run`/`reset`, tunes per-step `step_<n>_ticks`/`step_<n>_out` through `set_parameter`, and carries an in-flight sequence across checkpoint and promotion (#134, #82, decision 20); `valve` and `motor` carry command-with-feedback and the proven `fault`/`discrepancy` flags (#52); `bool-gate`/`sr-latch`/`interlock` compose Bool patterns and Float gating (#38, #52, #152); `rate-limiter` covers ramped rates and the graduated return-to-service [1 §6.3.a.12, 13, 17] (#85); `manual-station`/`override-select` cover operator takeover of a demand (#110); `totalizer` covers delivered-volume accounting (#111); and variable-arity bound-port discovery is the `in_N`/`trip_N` convention (#38, #152).
- **Problem:** The note's gaps 1, 2, and 7. The canonical backwash is timed cleaning phases bounded by measured-state steps — drain-down ends on level reached [20], wash phases on declared durations [9, 10, 12, 13], filter-to-waste on a turbidity and/or volume bound [4, 12, 15] — while `sequencer` advances on ticks alone (gap 1). Each phase drives a plant-specific combination of valves, pump, and blower while `sequencer.out` is one Float (gap 2) [10, 12, 23]. The sequence must start only when the arbiter grants the shared supply and must release the grant on completion or abort (gap 7). And the phase-internal guards the note names — never air scour together with high-rate wash [15], the drain-down filtration-rate hold [8] — are interlock-like constraints inside the step pattern, not just ordering.
- **Chosen approach (proposed):** A dedicated `backwash-sequence` kind rather than a grown `sequencer` — measured advance, the grant handshake, fault and abort steps, and trigger attribution are sequence-domain contract a general timed table should not carry, and `sequencer` stays the plain substrate simpler sequences keep. The kind walks a declared step table in `sequencer`'s shape — `step_count` plus per-step entries — extended per the gaps:
  *Measured-state advance* (gap 1): each step declares `step_<n>_advance` (`Int` code — `0` timed on `step_<n>_ticks` alone, the implemented `sequencer` rule; `1` measured — advance when the selected measured input crosses `step_<n>_bound`, `step_<n>_ticks` standing as the declared overrun timeout; `2` whichever comes first — measured bound or ticks). Measured steps name their input through `step_<n>_meas` (`Int` — selecting among the kind's indexed `meas_1`…`meas_K` `Float` inputs, discovered from bound names), so drain level, effluent turbidity, and delivered bed volume [4, 12, 20] land as ordinary signals. A measured step overrunning its tick bound takes the declared `step_<n>_on_overrun` behavior (`Int` code — `0` advance anyway; `1` hold and raise `overrun`) — the timeout semantics the note requires but no source fixes, so the code set is proposed and the per-plant choice assumption-marked. `overrun` (`Out`, `Bool`) asserts while a measured step stands past its bound — the surface decision 61's alarm set consumes.
  *Per-step equipment pattern* (gap 2): the kind emits declared per-phase outputs — `phase_<n>` (`Out`, `Bool`, one per declared step, generated from `step_count`) asserting while step `n` is active — beside `step` (`Out`, `Int`) and `out` (`Out`, `Float`) driving `step_<n>_out` for rate-valued phases, as `sequencer` already does. Each valve, pump, and blower command then composes as a `bool-gate` OR over the phase flags that drive it, and each rate demand as `out` gated by its phase flag through `override-select`/`interlock` — plant-specific equipment wiring, never kind vocabulary, the division decisions 41 and 51 record. Phase-internal guards land two ways: single-active-step semantics structurally exclude cross-phase pairs — air scour and high-rate wash declared as distinct steps can never assert together — and a declared `interlock` on the high-rate demand path tripped by the air-scour phase flag guards a decode-table error, the belt-and-suspenders form of the [15] exclusion; the drain-down rate hold [8] composes as a `rate-limiter`-bounded demand wired through the drain-down phase flag.
  *Grant-gated start and release* (gap 7): `request` (`Out`, `Bool`) asserts from the first armed trigger (decision 58) until the sequence completes or aborts, wired to the coordinator's `request_i`; `grant` (`In`, `Bool`), wired from `grant_i`, is the run permissive — the table advances only while granted, so a queued filter sits armed on its first step with `request` standing. `done` (`Out`, `Bool`) asserts at table end and `aborted` (`Out`, `Bool`) on the decision-60 paths; either drops `request`, which is the grant release — no separate release protocol. `active` (`Out`, `Bool`) reports stepping-under-grant, and `request`/`active` supply the meanwhile-state conditions decision 56's `queued_state` selects between: `0` gates the filter's in-service path on `active` — keep filtering until the wash starts — and `1` on `request` — offline once queued, the standby cover composed in bank wiring [8, 9]; the gating itself is an `interlock` trip on the in-service demand, so the declared choice lands as one wiring difference the bank helper emits from the declared parameter. Non-`Good` inputs take the fail-safe reading — a `grant` that cannot be proven holds the table; a `meas_i` that cannot be trusted cannot satisfy its step's bound, so the declared ticks/overrun rule governs — the implementing ticket documents the precise rules.
  Checkpoint obligations: the active step, banked count, armed request and held trigger, and overrun state are run state under decision 20's rule — the 60–75-minute cycle [12] crossing a promotion is the note's recorded `WW-LCM-001` reach, a proposal the sources do not address.
- **Alternatives considered:** Extending `sequencer` with per-step measured advance (rejected — the extension is the smaller half of this contract; grant gating, fault and abort steps, trigger attribution, and `aborted` would still need a backwash-aware home, and loading them onto the general kind splits one contract across two — the note's gap-1 "or" resolves to the dedicated kind once gaps 5–7 land beside it); an Int `step` code decoded to per-phase conditions by external comparators (no Int-equality kind exists — banding `threshold-chain` per step is the only composable decode, an indirection the generated `phase_<n>` outputs remove while keeping the equipment mapping in wiring where the plant-specific data lives); declared per-equipment outputs on the kind (freezes one equipment vocabulary — valve set, pump count, blower — where the evidence shows the pattern is plant-specific [10, 12, 23]); the phase guards as engineering review only (the [15] air-plus-high-rate exclusion is a safety-shaped constraint the contract must make structurally expressible — single-active-step plus the declared interlock give it two independent enforcements).
- **Consequences:** One kind carries the sequence contract — timed and measured advance, the grant handshake, the phase-flag surface, and the `aborted`/`overrun`/`trigger_source` status vocabulary `sequencer` lacks — while the equipment pattern and guards stay declared, checkable wiring. `sequencer` itself is untouched; the measured-advance need is answered by the new kind and non-backwash sequences keep the simpler contract. The `Int`-coded per-step vocabulary (`advance`, `meas`, `on_overrun`) is recorded honestly, and every assumption-marked element — the client's step vocabulary, which steps end measured versus timed, the overrun responses — stays flagged. The `dcs-build` spec obligation and checkpoint coverage follow every kind's convention.
- **Affected tickets:** #253 (this record); the M10 `backwash-sequence` kind ticket — the kind plus its `dcs-build` spec under the drift guard (to be filed); the M10 filter-bank composition ticket carrying the phase-decode and guard wiring (to be filed); the scripted acceptance-slice ticket exercising measured advance, overrun, and the grant handshake (to be filed); requirement `WW-CTL-004`; relies on #134, #52, #38, #152, #85, #110, #111, #82 and decisions 11, 16, 20, 41, 51; evidence `docs/research/filter-backwash.md` (#233) sources [4, 8, 9, 10, 12, 13, 15, 17, 20, 23].

### 58. Backwash triggers, auto-start permission, and attribution

- **Status:** Accepted — a proposed contract on implemented seams. Implemented basis: `timer`/`counter` express elapsed run time and filtered volume (#85); `analog-input` plus `alarm-monitor`/`threshold-chain` express headloss and effluent-turbidity thresholds (#11, #22, #211); `edge-trigger` converts levels to edges (#152); `bool-gate` ORs conditions (#152); `sr-latch` holds an armed condition (#152); writable internal `In` points carry the operator-start request through the receipted, attributed path (#30, #49, #165, decisions 14, 18, 39).
- **Problem:** The declared trigger set — elapsed run time, terminal headloss, effluent turbidity, operator start — is owner-convergent [3, 8, 9, 12, 17, 18, 20, 21], but two things are not universal: whether automatic triggers may *start* a wash is jurisdiction-mandated in both directions ([3] requires turbidity-initiated automatic backwash; [1 §6.3.a.12, 7] require operator initiation), and owners require the firing trigger recorded per backwash [8] — the note's gap 4, since a `bool-gate` OR emits the union, not which input fired. The decision: which seams express the trigger primaries, where auto-start permission is declared, and how attribution lands.
- **Chosen approach (proposed):** The primaries compose upstream — `timer` on the filter's in-service state for run time, `alarm-monitor`/`threshold-chain` flags on headloss and effluent-turbidity analog inputs, a writable internal `In` point for operator start — each landing on its own named input on `backwash-sequence`: `trig_time`, `trig_headloss`, `trig_turbidity`, `trig_operator` (`In`, `Bool`). Separate inputs, not a pre-OR'd condition, because the kind owns attribution: `trigger_source` (`Out`, `Int` — `0` none, `1` time, `2` headloss, `3` turbidity, `4` operator) captures the first asserted source when the request arms and holds it until the backwash's record completes — the journaled status transition plus point history are the per-backwash record [8] requires, no new journal event type (gap 4 resolved as a declared output, not derivable after the fact).
  Auto-start permission is declared data on the kind — `auto_start` (`Int` code — `0` an automatic trigger's armed request asserts `request` directly, the [3]/[8] direction; `1` an automatic trigger arms and latches a pending state only `trig_operator` releases to `request`, the [1 §6.3.a.12, 7] direction) — declared per plant because the evidence mandates both directions; the first client's answer is the note's recorded assumption. `pending` (`Out`, `Bool`) reports the armed-but-held state so the operator sees a request awaiting their start; `trig_operator` under `auto_start = 0` queues a wash unconditionally — the natural reading, the implementing ticket documents it.
- **Alternatives considered:** A pre-OR'd `trigger` input with the set kept in wiring (loses attribution — gap 4's whole point; the kind cannot say which source fired); attribution reconstructed from per-source point history (the record would live outside the contract — the sources require the firing trigger as the sequence's observable record [8], and a derived reconstruction cannot distinguish simultaneous assertions from a race); auto-start permission as plant wiring — an `sr-latch` on the OR'd triggers AND-ed with operator start (expressible, but it buries a regulator-mandated behavior in ad-hoc wiring where the mandate differs by jurisdiction; a declared parameter makes the permission checkable contract data — the same reason `on_bad_flow` is a parameter in decision 50); an indexed `trigger_i` input set with per-plant source naming (the four-source set is the convergent vocabulary — named inputs self-describe in the descriptor where opaque indices would not).
- **Consequences:** The trigger primaries need no contract work; the two genuinely jurisdictional behaviors — auto-start permission and source attribution — land as a declared parameter and a declared output on the sequence kind. The operator-start input is the same writable-point seam every operator command uses, so a manual start is receipted, attributed, and journaled like any command. Which sources a plant declares, the `auto_start` answer, and whether the turbidity tier additionally drives the 1 NTU shutdown path [3] stay assumption-marked.
- **Affected tickets:** #253 (this record); the M10 `backwash-sequence` kind ticket carries the trigger inputs, `auto_start`, and `trigger_source` (to be filed); the M10 filter-bank composition ticket wires the primaries (to be filed); the scripted acceptance-slice ticket asserts each trigger's attribution (to be filed); requirement `WW-CTL-004`; relies on #85, #11, #22, #211, #152, #30, #49, #165 and decisions 14, 18, 39; evidence `docs/research/filter-backwash.md` (#233) sources [1, 3, 7, 8, 9, 12, 17, 18, 20, 21].

### 59. The backwash operator command surface

- **Status:** Accepted — proposed; the carried surface is evidenced, the rest assumption-marked. Implemented basis: writable internal `In` points carry operator commands through the receipted, actor-attributed `WriteValue` path with named rejections (#30, #49, #165, decisions 7, 14, 18, 39); `set_parameter` tunes descriptor-declared parameters at the scan boundary (#82, decision 20) — the per-phase rate/duration tuning the owner evidence requires [7, 10, 15]; `manual-station`/`override-select` cover operator takeover of analog demands and equipment source selection (#110).
- **Problem:** The note's gap 5 — which of start, abort-to-a-declared-safe-step, hold, advance, and manual-step the contract carries. The owner evidence is consistent on start [3, 7, 9, 12, 13, 21] and abort [10], plus per-phase rate/duration tuning and valves-to-auto/manual requests [7, 10, 15]; hold/advance/manual-step appear only in integrator practice [23], not owner documents — so the surface must be recorded with the evidenced half carried and the unevidenced half honestly open.
- **Chosen approach (proposed):** The contract carries *start* and *abort*; *hold*, *advance*, and *manual-step* are recorded open customer-validation questions with their seam named, not silently omitted.
  - *Start* — the `trig_operator` input (decision 58) bound to a writable internal `In` point: the receipted write arms or releases the request per `auto_start`, attributed and journaled [3, 7, 9, 12, 13, 21].
  - *Abort* — a declared `abort` (`In`, `Bool`) input bound to a writable internal `In` point; asserting it drives the sequence to its declared `abort_step` and raises `aborted` — the Tulsa object's `BW_Abort_CMD`/`BW_Aborted` pair [10] — receipted and attributed like every operator action.
  - *Queue reorder* — the coordinator's reorder input (decision 56), a writable internal point where the plant exposes reorder authority — itself an assumption-marked choice.
  - *Rate and duration tuning* — the per-step `step_<n>_ticks`/`step_<n>_out` parameters tune through `set_parameter` under descriptor-declared ranges, the implemented convention `sequencer` already follows [7, 10, 15]; equipment-level override — valves to manual, bank auto/manual requests [10] — composes per equipment through `manual-station`/`override-select` and the `valve`/`motor` wiring the station pattern uses (decisions 41–42).
  - *Hold / advance / manual-step* — recorded open assumptions: [23] is integrator practice, not owner evidence. If the customer answers yes, the declared shape lands as further `In` inputs (`hold`, `advance`, `step_select`) on the same receipted-point convention — no contract or transport work — and this decision reopens; the implementing ticket does not build them on spec.
- **Alternatives considered:** Carrying the full integrator surface now (invents operator authority no owner source requires — decision 54's identical reasoning for diurnal dosing); abort as a `reset` equivalent (a bare reset abandons the bed mid-state — the evidence's abort drives to a declared safe step with an `aborted` indication [10], which `sequencer.reset` cannot express); a dedicated command variant beside `WriteValue`/`set_parameter` (rejected with decisions 7 and 33 — point writes on declared inputs are the established journaled surface, and a new variant adds vocabulary without semantics); the operator surface through a `run`/`reset` idiom alone (holds and restarts exist but express neither abort-to-step nor the attributed start the receipts must carry).
- **Consequences:** The carried surface is exactly the owner-evidenced vocabulary — start, abort, reorder where declared, tuning — all through the receipted path under existing conventions, so backwash adds no command machinery. The unevidenced commands stay visible as assumptions with a named landing seam, so a customer "yes" is an additive input set, not a contract change.
- **Affected tickets:** #253 (this record); the M10 `backwash-sequence` kind ticket carries `abort` (to be filed); the M10 `backwash-coordinator` ticket carries the reorder input (to be filed); the filter-bank composition ticket binds the writable points (to be filed); the scripted acceptance-slice ticket exercises start, abort, reorder, and tuning with their receipts (to be filed); requirement `WW-CTL-004`; relies on #30, #49, #165, #82, #110 and decisions 7, 14, 18, 20, 39, 54; evidence `docs/research/filter-backwash.md` (#233) sources [3, 7, 9, 10, 12, 13, 15, 21, 23].

### 60. Mid-sequence failure — the declared fault step and abort semantics

- **Status:** Accepted — proposed contract on implemented seams. Implemented basis: `valve`/`motor` prove equipment failure — the `fault`/`discrepancy` flags after declared disagreeing scans (#52); `sequencer`'s hold-on-non-`Good`-input rule is the fail-safe reading convention (#134); the coordinator releases the grant when `request` drops (decision 56); `bool-latching-alarm` carries the fault and aborted alarms (#219, decision 43).
- **Problem:** The note's gap 6 — a proven equipment fault mid-sequence, a documented hazard [22], needs a declared `on_fault` step and a declared resume-versus-abort policy, and `aborted` must stand distinct from `done` per the Tulsa vocabulary [10]. `sequencer` has no fault input and no fault step. The sources publish no uniform rule — which step a failed valve or blower returns the filter to, whether the sequence resumes after repair or aborts and re-queues, whether the aborted filter returns to service — so these are contract parameters the customer answers, not defaults.
- **Chosen approach (proposed):** `backwash-sequence` takes `fault` (`In`, `Bool` — the per-filter equipment-fault aggregate, composed upstream from the participating `valve`/`motor` `fault`/`discrepancy` flags through `bool-gate` OR; where per-phase fault relevance is declared, the aggregation composes from the phase flags) plus declared parameters `on_fault_step` (`Int` — the step a proven fault drives the sequence to: a hold, a drain-to-safe step, or an abort step — inside the safe directions the evidence implies: bed covered, never air plus high-rate water, the filter held out of service, the failure alarmed [10, 11, 15]) and `on_fault_policy` (`Int` code — `0` hold at the fault step pending operator action, resume landing on the receipted start/abort vocabulary; `1` abort: drive `abort_step`, assert `aborted`, drop `request` so the grant releases and the filter may re-queue on a fresh trigger). `aborted` (`Out`, `Bool`) is a distinct status from `done` [10] — a completed wash and a faulted one are different events the alarm set and journal record differently. Whether a held fault step keeps the grant or releases it immediately is the implementing ticket's documented detail under the declared policy; whether the aborted filter returns to service without a completed wash is the note's recorded assumption.
- **Alternatives considered:** Fault handling left to per-plant wiring (the failed-equipment → safe-step transition is per-scan sequence state — wiring can gate inputs but cannot reposition a step table, and leaving it out makes every bank's worst case a bespoke answer); a fixed abort-always policy (the evidence shows owners require override and safe containment but leave resume-versus-abort to the plant's control narrative — a fixed choice silently decides what must be declared); resume-in-place as the default (assumes repair semantics no source states); the operator abort as the fault path (a proven fault must reach the safe step without waiting on an operator [22]).
- **Consequences:** The fault path is declared data — which step, which policy — so the acceptance scenario can assert it and the customer answers it; `aborted`/`done`/`active`/`overrun`/`pending` give the bank the status vocabulary the alarm set (decision 61) and the journaled events consume. The grant interaction is pinned: abort drops `request` and releases the supply for the queue; the fault-step-holding-grant sub-case is recorded as the ticket's documented choice rather than silently settled.
- **Affected tickets:** #253 (this record); the M10 `backwash-sequence` kind ticket carries `fault`, `on_fault_step`, `on_fault_policy`, `abort_step`, and `aborted` (to be filed); the filter-bank composition ticket wires the fault aggregate (to be filed); the scripted acceptance-slice ticket drives a mid-sequence fault and asserts the declared step, `aborted`, and the grant release (to be filed); requirement `WW-CTL-004`; relies on #52, #134, #219 and decisions 20, 43; evidence `docs/research/filter-backwash.md` (#233) sources [10, 11, 15, 22].

### 61. Post-wash verification and the filter-bank alarm set

- **Status:** Accepted — the lifecycle and the `phase-monitor` kind are implemented; the set's mapping composition is proposed. Implemented basis: `latching-alarm` (#109) and `bool-latching-alarm` (#219) carry the two-flag lifecycle — `alarm` standing, `unacknowledged` latched until the model-wired writable `ack` point reads true, acks journaled through the ordinary settled receipt, the latch carried in checkpoints (decisions 33, 38, 43); `alarm-monitor` thresholds Floats (#22); the journal and point history record transitions and settled commands (#35, #163, decisions 17, 36); the phase flags and status outputs of decisions 56–57 supply the conditions; `phase-monitor` lands the phase gate, captured baseline, and deadline below (#266).
- **Problem:** The note's gaps 8 and 9. The post-wash checks are phase-conditioned comparisons `alarm-monitor` cannot express ungated: clean-bed headloss within a declared deviation of its captured baseline at a given flow [8], and ripening turbidity under declared bounds within declared durations of return to service [4, 8, 15]. And the declared bank alarm set — the effluent-turbidity tiers [1 §6.3.a.11, 3, 6], terminal headloss [8, 12, 18], trigger-attributed start events [8], aborted/failed backwash [10], the resource-blocked queue, online-flow disturbance [8], CBHL and ripening excursions [8, 15], the per-class equipment minimums [11] — must map onto the two-flag lifecycle; gap 9 records that no new alarm contract is needed once the coordinator and sequence expose their status outputs.
- **Chosen approach (proposed):** The phase-conditioned checks land as one small kind — `phase-monitor`: `in` (`In`, `Float` — the measured value), `phase` (`In`, `Bool` — the condition window, wired from the decoded phase flags or the return-to-service state), `capture` (`In`, `Bool` — while asserted the kind holds `in` as the baseline, the CBHL "original at a given flow" capture [8]); `deviation` (`Out`, `Float`), `exceeded` (`Out`, `Bool` — the excursion condition the alarm set consumes), `overdue` (`Out`, `Bool` — a bound not met within `limit_ticks` of the phase opening). Parameters `bound` (`Float` — the absolute bound or maximum deviation), `limit_ticks` (`Int` — the duration bound), and `mode` (`Int` code — `0` absolute bound on `in`; `1` deviation from the captured baseline) under the decision-41/42 descriptor conventions. The composition alternative cannot express it: `alarm-monitor` bounds against static limits while this check needs a phase gate, a value capture, and a deadline — `interlock`-gating the measured value to a benign `safe_value` outside the phase works for gating alone but buries the check's semantics in wiring and still cannot express "below bound within duration" or a held baseline. Ripening composes as `phase` wired to the post-wash condition with `mode 0`, the declared NTU bound, and `limit_ticks` the 30-minute-cap class deadline [4, 8, 15]; CBHL as `capture` on the reference-flow step then `mode 1` deviation monitoring [8]. `phase-monitor`'s captured baseline and timers are run state under decision 20's rule; the kind ships its `dcs-build` spec under the drift guard.
  The alarm set, each element named to its seam:
  - *Effluent turbidity tiers* — the 0.3 NTU per-filter alarm and the 1 NTU shutdown tier [1 §6.3.a.11, 3, 6]: `latching-alarm` instances on the turbidity Float; whether the 1 NTU tier is alarm-only or additionally drives the filter offline is jurisdiction-dependent [3] — assumption-marked, and where it drives, the tier also feeds the sequence's `fault` wiring.
  - *Terminal headloss* — `latching-alarm` on the headloss Float [8, 12, 18], its flag available to the trigger wiring (decision 58).
  - *Trigger-attributed start and backwash status events* — the sequence's `trigger_source`, `active`, `done`, and `aborted` transitions journal through the existing recorder — point history plus the transition journal, no new event types (gap 9); `aborted` additionally feeds `bool-latching-alarm` where the plant alarms it [10].
  - *Resource-blocked queue* — the coordinator's `resource_blocked` into `bool-latching-alarm`.
  - *Online-flow disturbance* — `latching-alarm` on the bank flow-disturbance measurement against the declared bound [8], the same signal the `flow_ok` grant permissive consumes.
  - *CBHL and ripening excursions* — `phase-monitor`'s `exceeded`/`overdue` into `bool-latching-alarm` [8, 15]; the monthly-aggregate target [8] is a history/journal-derived reporting obligation, not a trip alarm — recorded rather than silently absent.
  - *Equipment faults* — the `valve`/`motor` `fault`/`discrepancy` flags per actuator, blower (a `motor` instance), pump, and valve [11] into `bool-latching-alarm`, simultaneously feeding the sequence's `fault` aggregate.
  - *Record-keeping obligations* — documented consistent use of alternate ripening techniques [4] and the no-unsettled-recycle rule [3] ride the journal and history, not the alarm path.
  Which alarms latch until acknowledged versus auto-clear is per-alarm model wiring — `latching-alarm`/`bool-latching-alarm` versus `alarm-monitor` — and the required latch set is the note's recorded customer-validation question, assumption-marked.
- **Alternatives considered:** `alarm-monitor` instances gated by `interlock` safe-values (expresses phase-gating only as a wiring trick — no capture, no deadline, and the check's meaning disappears into parameter values: the unverifiable-composition failure these kind decisions exist to avoid); two separate kinds for CBHL and ripening (one shape covers both — a bounded comparison with an optional captured baseline and an optional deadline; splitting duplicates the contract); a dedicated bank-alarm kind or vocabulary (rejected with decisions 43 and 55 — every element is a standing condition plus a latch the existing kinds already speak); bank events as new journal event types (rejected with decision 33 — status-output transitions already journal).
- **Consequences:** One small kind plus wired instances carry the whole verification-and-alarm surface; the two-flag lifecycle is untouched and acknowledgment needs no new path — gap 9's prediction confirmed. The assumption-marked items — the latch set, the 1 NTU tier's alarm-versus-trip scope, the monthly-aggregate reporting form — stay flagged for customer validation.
- **Affected tickets:** #253 (this record); the M10 `phase-monitor` kind ticket — the kind plus its `dcs-build` spec under the drift guard (to be filed); the M10 filter-bank composition ticket wiring the alarm set (to be filed); the scripted acceptance-slice ticket asserting each raised alarm and the journaled events (to be filed); requirements `WW-CTL-004` and `WW-OPS-002`; relies on #109, #219, #22, #35, #163 and decisions 17, 20, 33, 36, 38, 43; evidence `docs/research/filter-backwash.md` (#233) sources [1, 3, 4, 6, 8, 10, 11, 12, 15, 18].

### 62. Shared-header coordination — the aeration coordinator

- **Status:** Accepted — the contract below is proposed; the M10 implementation tickets (to be filed) land it, and the note requires this question recorded before any implementation ticket dispatches. Implemented basis it builds on: `pid` + `analog-input` + `signal-filter` express the per-zone DO and airflow loops the coordinator feeds (#11, #110); `valve` carries the modulating air valve with `fb` position feedback and the proven `discrepancy` flag (#52); `rate-limiter` bounds a demand's slew — the gradual-adjustment requirement [7 §3.4] (#85); `timer`/`counter` express the per-grid mixing timer (#85); `pump-group` is the indexed per-member port precedent — bound-name-discovered `cmd_i`/`run_i` families and checkpointed group state (#210, decision 41) — and `backwash-coordinator` the many-input coordination precedent: per-scan stateful policy in a kind, construction-time composition in `dcs-build` (decision 56, #112, #151, decision 31); the descriptor and checkpoint conventions every kind follows are implemented (decisions 16, 11, 20).
- **Problem:** The note's gap 1 — the central contract question — plus gap 6 folded in. Independent per-zone DO→valve loops fight through the shared air header: raising airflow to one basin drops it in another and the controllers hunt [4 §6.3.3.1, 18]. The evidence shows three coordination remedies, all owner-specified: constant discharge header pressure while zones throttle [5 §4.3.1, 16]; most-open-valve pressure reset, the set-point lowered until the most-open zone valve sits in a declared near-open band such as 85–95% [4 §6.3.3.1, 5 §4.3.1/Case 6, 18, 19]; and direct-airflow control, coordination manipulating the valve positions themselves so one valve is always at maximum with no pressure loop [5 Case Study 6]. Whatever the strategy, the result must feed a total air demand split across the running blowers [4 §6.3.3.1, 6 §2.2.1.11.6]. Nothing existing expresses any of it: no kind computes a max-of-N selection or a set-point reset a wiring convention could fake, `pump-group` stages on/off members rather than optimizing a shared continuous resource, and `dcs-build` builder composition holds no per-scan run state. Gap 6's plant-wide cap on simultaneously pulsing grids [7 §3.4] is a cross-instance constraint of the same shape — a per-grid timer cannot see the count — so it folds into this decision.
- **Chosen approach (proposed):** A new `header-coordinator` kind in `dcs-blocks` plus a `dcs-build` builder-level aeration-train composition helper — the decision-41/56 split applied a third time: the most-open selection, the walking pressure set-point, and the pulse-grant set are per-scan run state, so they belong in an executor component, while the train as one typed artifact — N zones, the coordinator, the blower group — is construction-time composition on decision 31's seam.
  Declared I/O (proposed; per-zone ports indexed `_1`…`_N` under the `trip_N`/`in_N`/`request_i` convention, the zone count discovered from bound port names): per zone `i`, `valve_pos_i` (`In`, `Float` — the zone valve's position feedback, normally the same field point the `valve` reads), `airflow_i` (`In`, `Float` — the zone's airflow demand, the DO/airflow loop's output the header must deliver [4 §6.3.3.1]), `pulsing_i` (`In`, `Bool` — the grid's mixing-pulse request, composed per grid in `timer`-driven wiring [7 §3.4]), and `pulse_grant_i` (`Out`, `Bool` — the coordinator's pulse admission); bank-level `pressure` (`In`, `Float` — the discharge-header pressure transmitter [7 §3.4, 4 §6.5.2.3]), `pressure_sp` (`Out`, `Float` — the header-pressure set-point the blower capacity loop tracks), `blower_demand` (`Out`, `Float` — the aggregate capacity demand decision 63's group stages against), `most_open` (`Out`, `Int` — the 1-based index of the zone whose valve is most open, `0` while none is trusted), `at_bound` (`Out`, `Bool` — the set-point or demand resting at a declared bound: the coordinator can no longer optimize, the control-health indication decision 69 alarms), and `pulse_blocked` (`Out`, `Bool` — a pulse request standing refused by the declared cap).
  Parameters (proposed — `Int` codes where `Value` has no enum, the decision-41/50 convention): `strategy` (`Int` — `0` constant header pressure: `pressure_sp` holds the declared `pressure_hold` [5 §4.3.1, 16]; `1` most-open-valve reset: `pressure_sp` walks until the most-open `valve_pos_i` settles inside the declared `mov_band_lo`–`mov_band_hi` band, the 85–95% practice carried as declared data rather than a fixed band [19]; `2` direct-airflow: `blower_demand` sums the zone `airflow_i` demands and no pressure set-point is emitted, the Bucklin Point retrofit form [5 Case Study 6]); `pressure_hold` (`Float`); `pressure_min`/`pressure_max` (`Float` — the declared header bounds, an efficiency bound and a protection boundary both [4 §6.5.1.4, 16]); `mov_band_lo`/`mov_band_hi` (`Float`); `adjust_ticks` (`Int` — the minimum interval between set-point moves, the tick-domain form of the 5–30-minute adjustment cadence [4 §5.4.3.1], with a downstream `rate-limiter` bounding the slew per [7 §3.4]); `min_total_airflow` (`Float` — the header-level mixing floor [4 §6.3.3.1]); `max_pulsing` (`Int` — the declared cap on simultaneously pulsing grids, the "controller-computed maximum" carried as declared data [7 §3.4]: the coordinator admits `pulsing_i` requests up to the cap and asserts `pulse_blocked` over the rest). The `strategy` choice, the band, the bounds, and whether pulsed-aeration mixing is required at all are the note's recorded customer-validation assumptions carried as declared data, never kind defaults.
  Behavior: `most_open` ignores non-`Good` or non-finite `valve_pos_i` — an untrusted valve cannot claim the band; a non-`Good` `pressure` under strategy 1 holds the last emitted set-point — the implementing ticket documents the precise rules under the library's fail-safe reading convention.
  Descriptor roles: `valve_pos_i`/`airflow_i`/`pulsing_i`/`pressure` → `ProcessValue`; `pressure_sp`/`blower_demand`/`pulse_grant_i` → `Output`; `most_open`/`at_bound`/`pulse_blocked` → `Status`.
  Checkpoint obligations: the emitted set-point and demand, the held pulse-grant set, and the most-open identity are run state captured and restored under decision 20's rule — a promotion mid-adjustment must not step the header pressure (the note's recorded `WW-LCM-001` reach; sources do not address controller redundancy — assumption-marked).
- **Alternatives considered:** A documented wiring convention (rejected per the note's gap 1 — max-of-N selection and the set-point walk have no fold into existing kinds, and a convention gives every train its own unverifiable coordination); `dcs-build` builder-level composition emitting the coordination as model data (rejected — construction-time wiring cannot hold a walking set-point or a grant set, decision 41's identical reasoning); folding coordination into `pump-group` (its members are on/off stage counts — the header problem is continuous optimization over valve positions and pressure); one coordinator kind per strategy (the three forms share the selection, bound, and pulse machinery — a declared `strategy` parameter keeps them one contract and records the choice as checkable data, the same reason `on_bad_flow` is a parameter in decision 50); the pulse cap left to per-grid wiring (a per-grid `timer` cannot see the plant-wide count — gap 6's whole point, and the coordinator already sees every zone).
- **Consequences:** One kind carries the header contract — the declared strategy and its bounds, the demand split surface, and the folded pulse cap — while the zone loops stay the implemented `pid`/`valve` composition and every assumption-marked element (primary strategy, band, bounds, the pulse requirement) stays flagged for customer validation rather than silently defaulting. The `Int`-coded vocabulary is recorded honestly per decisions 41 and 50; the kind ships its `dcs-build` spec under the drift guard and its checkpoint vocabulary deploys to both peers under decision 11's strictness.
- **Affected tickets:** #254 (this record); the M10 `header-coordinator` kind ticket — the kind plus its `dcs-build` spec under the drift guard (to be filed); the M10 aeration-train composition ticket — the builder-level train helper and the checked-in fixture (to be filed); the scripted acceptance-slice ticket (to be filed); requirement `WW-CTL-005`; relies on #52, #85, #110, #210, #112, #151 and decisions 11, 16, 20, 31, 41, 56; evidence `docs/research/aeration-do-control.md` (#241) sources [4, 5, 7, 16, 18, 19].

### 63. Blower-group staging semantics

- **Status:** Accepted — the contract below is proposed; the M10 implementation tickets (to be filed) land it. Implemented basis it builds on: `pump-group` implements the group skeleton — indexed `cmd_i`/`run_i`/`fault_i`/`avail_i`, the `duty`/`staged`/`none_available`/`all_faulted` status family, declared rotation and timing parameters, checkpointed group state (#210, decision 41); `sequencer` walks a declared tick-timed step table (#134); `motor` + `analog-output` carry the per-machine actuation halves (decisions 52, 67); `bool-gate`/`sr-latch`/`interlock` compose Bool conditions and Float gating (#38, #52, #152); writable internal `In` points carry the operator stage-approval through the receipted, attributed path (#30, #49, #165, decisions 14, 18, 39).
- **Problem:** The note's gap 2. Blower staging is policy plus machinery constraints — the number of blowers online is selected to meet demand [6 §2.2.1.11.6] — but the demand is continuous: a capacity split or set-point per running machine against per-unit min/max flow and current bounds, stage-up/stage-down thresholds that use each blower's full range to minimize starts [4 §6.5.1.2], minimum run time and time between starts [4 §6.5.1.2], the N+1 availability selection the standards require [1 §92.332.e, 2, 3(a)], and lead/standby alternation [16]. Each join is a documented choreography — start offline through a vent/recycle valve, sometimes reduce header pressure so its check valve opens, sometimes trim running blowers first [4 §6.5.1.2] — and rotation runs a valve-freeze/pressure-relief transition [4 §6.6.2]. `pump-group`'s `demand` is an `Int` stage count over on/off members; it carries none of the continuous or sequenced parts.
- **Chosen approach (proposed):** A new `blower-group` kind — the pump-group precedent extended for continuous capacity, recorded as a sibling rather than a `pump-group` extension because the demand type and the join choreography differ.
  Declared I/O (proposed; per-unit ports indexed `_1`…`_N` under the established convention): `demand` (`In`, `Float` — the aggregate capacity demand, wired from the coordinator's `blower_demand`); per unit `i`, `cmd_i` (`Out`, `Bool` — the run request to the unit's `motor.cmd`), `run_i`/`fault_i`/`avail_i` (`In`, `Bool` — the pump-group family, availability aggregated upstream in declared wiring per decision 41), `capacity_i` (`Out`, `Float` — the running unit's capacity demand — its share of `demand` under the declared split, clamped to the unit's declared bounds — feeding the speed/vane demand path through decision 64's guard), and `vent_i` (`Out`, `Bool` — the unloading/vent valve command for the offline start [4 §6.5.1.2, 6]); status `staged` (`Out`, `Int`), `none_available`/`all_faulted` (`Out`, `Bool` — the pump-group vocabulary), `staging_pending` (`Out`, `Bool` — a stage-up or stage-down held for operator approval [4 §6.5.1.2]), and `transition` (`Out`, `Bool` — a start, stop, or rotation transition in progress — the surface the valve-freeze wiring consumes).
  Parameters (proposed — `Int` codes under the decision-41 convention): `staging_authority` (`Int` — `0` fully automatic stage-up/down [6 §2.2.1.11.6, 15]; `1` operator-approval required — `staging_pending` asserts until a writable internal approve point releases it; `2` flag-only — the request flags and the operation stays manual [4 §6.5.1.2]); `stage_up`/`stage_down` (`Float` — the declared demand thresholds); `min_run_ticks`/`min_start_interval_ticks` (`Int` — the minimum run time and time between starts [4 §6.5.1.2]); per-unit `unit_<i>_min_flow`/`unit_<i>_max_flow`/`unit_<i>_max_current` (`Float` — machine data, not library defaults [4 §6.5.1.2, 6]); `vent_ticks` (`Int` — the declared offline-vent dwell before a unit joins the header); `rotation` (`Int` code — the declared lead/standby alternation policy [16], the decision-41 `rotation` convention). The authority, rotation policy, and per-unit bounds are the note's recorded customer-validation assumptions.
  Choreography: the kind carries the declared per-unit join routine as internal state — `vent_i` opens, the unit runs to speed and proves, it joins the header, `vent_i` closes [4 §6.5.1.2, 6] — rather than a composed `sequencer` per machine, because the join is conditioned on live group state (header-pressure relief so the check valve opens, running units trimmed before the addition [4 §6.5.1.2, §6.6.2]) that per-machine wiring cannot see; the valve-freeze/pressure-relief rotation transition [4 §6.6.2] lands as the `transition` output bank wiring feeds into the coordinator or the zone demand path — the precise interaction is the implementing ticket's documented detail.
  Checkpoint obligations: the staging position, per-unit run-hours accumulators, in-flight min-run/start-interval timers, and any in-progress join or transition are run state under decision 20's rule — a promotion must not restart a half-joined unit or drop the rotation position.
- **Alternatives considered:** A declared `pump-group` extension plus a `sequencer` routine per unit (rejected — `pump-group`'s `demand` is an `Int` stage count; splitting a Float capacity demand changes the kind's core semantics, and a per-unit `sequencer` table cannot express join conditions on live group state); one `motor` + `sequencer` per blower composed in wiring (no home for the stage thresholds, the cross-group min-run enforcement, or the demand split — the unverifiable-composition failure the note's gap 1 names); folding the group into `header-coordinator` (the coordinator optimizes a shared continuous resource while the group stages discrete machines — two policies, two run-state sets, the decision-41 split keeping each in its own kind); staging left to operator discipline (owner systems stage automatically and the bounds are control behavior, not procedure [4 §6.5.1.2, 6, 15]; the flag-only authority remains a declared option, not the contract's shape).
- **Consequences:** One kind carries the staging contract — declared thresholds, per-unit bounds, authority, rotation, and the join choreography — while per-machine actuation stays the decision-52 composition. The `Int`-coded and per-unit-indexed parameter vocabulary is recorded honestly; every assumption-marked policy (authority, rotation, per-unit bounds) stays flagged for the customer answer, and the checkpoint vocabulary deploys to both peers under decision 11's strictness.
- **Affected tickets:** #254 (this record); the M10 `blower-group` kind ticket — the kind plus its `dcs-build` spec under the drift guard (to be filed); the M10 aeration-train composition ticket (to be filed); the scripted acceptance-slice ticket exercising stage-up, rotation, and a proven mid-start fault (to be filed); requirement `WW-CTL-005`; relies on #210, #134, #52, #38, #152, #165 and decisions 11, 16, 20, 41, 52; evidence `docs/research/aeration-do-control.md` (#241) sources [1, 2, 3, 4, 6, 15, 16].

### 64. The surge and machine-guard contract

- **Status:** Accepted — a proposed small kind; the M10 tickets (to be filed) land it. Implemented basis it builds on: `interlock` gates a Float `in` behind `permissive` plus `trip_1`…`trip_N` to a declared `safe_value`, fail-safe on non-`Good` inputs and auto-resetting (#38, #52); `bool-gate`/`sr-latch` compose Bool conditions and latched resets (#38, #152); `alarm-monitor`/`threshold-chain` express threshold flags (#22, #211); `motor` proves run-feedback faults (#52); the protective-device status set lands as debounced `digital-input` field points (#11); optional ports bind through `ComponentSpec::get` (the `flow-paced-ratio` `trim` convention, decision 50).
- **Problem:** The note's gap 3. The surge region is a two-variable flow-versus-pressure boundary guard on the demand path — protection the evidence calls essential, layered between the manufacturer's own system and the control algorithm [4 §6.5.1.4], with a minimum-amperage limit the common proxy [4 §6.5.1.4, §6.5.1.3]. "Clamp the demand above the declared minimum-flow/minimum-current bound and below the pressure bound, and trip on a proven surge" is a response `interlock` cannot express — its `safe_value` is a fixed fallback, not a bound on a moving demand, and it owns no proven-surge trip semantics. Meanwhile the machine's own protective devices — bearing over-temperature identifying the affected bearing, surge shutdown, vibration levels, motor overload, the PD-blower pressure relief valve, the startup/shutdown override [6 §2.2.1.11.x] — are hardwired equipment the contract must honor but never re-implement: they report status, and Madison showed what untied lockouts cost [4 §6.6.2].
- **Chosen approach (proposed):** A small `surge-guard` kind on the per-blower demand path — between the group's `capacity_i` and the machine's actuation demand — carrying the two-variable guard as declared data.
  Declared I/O (proposed): `demand` (`In`, `Float` — the unit's capacity demand), `flow` (`In`, `Float` — the unit's discharge airflow [16]), `pressure` (`In`, `Float` — the discharge or header pressure), `current` (`In`, `Float` — the motor current, the minimum-amperage surge proxy [4 §6.5.1.4, §6.5.1.3], optional through `ComponentSpec::get` so a unit without the signal declares no port), `surge_trip` (`In`, `Bool` — the machine's own proven-surge protective-device status, hardwired and only reporting [6 §2.2.1.11.x]); `out` (`Out`, `Float` — the guarded demand), `guarding` (`Out`, `Bool` — a bound engaged), `tripped` (`Out`, `Bool` — a proven surge or declared trip stands — the output decision 69's alarm set consumes).
  Parameters (proposed): `min_flow`/`max_pressure`/`min_current` (`Float` — the declared surge-region bounds, machine data [4 §6.5.1.4]); `on_guard` (`Int` code — `0` clamp `out` at the bound and continue, the minimum-flow maintenance [4 §6.5.1.4] prescribes; `1` trip — drive the declared `trip_value` and assert `tripped`); `trip_value` (`Float` — the demand a trip emits, the unload/vent direction the machine's shutdown requires). Whether a proven trip latches until reset or auto-clears is declared wiring — an `sr-latch` downstream where the owner requires it — the decision-51 latched-lockout division.
  Behavior: while `flow`, `pressure`, and a bound `current` sit outside the declared region the demand passes; crossing a bound engages `on_guard` with `guarding` asserted; a proven `surge_trip` asserts `tripped` unconditionally and drives `trip_value` — the hardwired device's status honored in the demand path. Non-`Good` guard inputs take the fail-safe reading — an untrusted flow cannot vouch the machine sits outside the region — the precise rules the implementing ticket documents. The rest of the protective set — bearing temperature, vibration, overload, oil system, override state — stays hardwired-and-reported: declared field `Bool` points feeding the unit's `avail_i`/`fault_i` aggregation and the alarm set, never re-implemented in the contract [6 §2.2.1.11.x].
- **Alternatives considered:** Declared bounds plus a surge-trip input carried on `blower-group` per unit (rejected — the guard is per-scan demand-path behavior with its own clamp state, and it serves a machine the group does not stage; folding it into the group couples protection semantics to staging policy and loses the standalone-composable form); `interlock` for the whole guard (its `safe_value` is a fixed fallback — "clamp at the bound and continue" is a different response it cannot express, and the two-variable region is not a Bool trip set); `alarm-monitor` bounds per variable (alarms without acting on the demand — the surge guard must move the demand, not only report [4 §6.5.1.4]); re-implementing machine protection in the contract (rejected outright — the devices are hardwired by specification [6 §2.2.1.11.x]; the contract's part is the algorithm layer and honoring the reported trips).
- **Consequences:** One small kind carries the algorithm-side surge layer; the protective trip set stays hardwired and reported, its signals landing on `surge_trip`, `avail_i`, and the alarm wiring. The declared bounds are machine data — assumption-marked per the note — and the latch policy follows the decision-43/51 wiring division rather than a kind mode.
- **Affected tickets:** #254 (this record); the M10 `surge-guard` kind ticket — the kind plus its `dcs-build` spec under the drift guard (to be filed); the M10 aeration-train composition ticket wiring it onto each unit's demand path (to be filed); the scripted acceptance-slice ticket driving a surge-region entry and a proven trip (to be filed); requirement `WW-CTL-005`; relies on #38, #52, #11, #22 and decisions 20, 43, 50, 51; evidence `docs/research/aeration-do-control.md` (#241) sources [4 §6.5.1.4, §6.5.1.3, §6.6.2, 6 §2.2.1.11.x, 16].

### 65. The declared DO-loss fallback

- **Status:** Accepted — a proposed small kind; the M10 tickets (to be filed) land it. Implemented basis it builds on: `median-voter` votes three probes with a spread `discrepancy` flag and worst-quality propagation — the voting form [19] recommends (#111); `failover-select` covers primary/backup probe selection with the alarmed `backup_active` (#211, decision 42); `threshold-chain`'s `on_bad_demand` is the declared all-bad-response precedent (#211, decision 42); `flow-paced-ratio`'s `on_bad_flow`/`fallback_rate`/`fallback_active` its demand-path form, landed (#263, decision 50); `bool-latching-alarm` carries the alarmed transition (#219, decision 43); `set_parameter` tunes descriptor-declared codes under ranges (#82, decision 20).
- **Problem:** The note's gap 4. When every DO source for a zone is non-`Good` the demand path needs a declared response — hold the last demand, revert to a fixed airflow, or drive to a declared safe airflow — alarmed on transition. The sources evidence the measurement-redundancy forms — probe select, averaging, backup controller, three-probe voting [7, 12, 19] — but publish no uniform terminal fallback (the note's recorded absence), so the response is declared-behavior data, not a library default. `pid` has no bad-measurement semantics: a non-`Good` `pv` propagates quality but no declared demand floor engages; `manual-station`'s `mode` is an operator input, not a quality response.
- **Chosen approach (proposed):** A small `demand-fallback` kind on the zone's DO→airflow demand path — the `threshold-chain.on_bad_demand`/`flow-paced-ratio.on_bad_flow` pattern applied at the loop's output rather than inside it, keeping `pid` untouched and the fallback reusable wherever a measurement-conditioned demand needs a declared answer.
  Declared I/O (proposed): `in` (`In`, `Float` — the loop's airflow demand), `pv` (`In`, `Float` — the zone's selected DO measurement: the voter's or selector's `out`, or the single probe); `out` (`Out`, `Float` — the demand served downstream), `fallback_active` (`Out`, `Bool` — the alarmed-engagement flag decision 69's set consumes).
  Parameters (proposed): `on_bad` (`Int` code — `0` hold the last `Good`-stamped demand; `1` drive the declared `fallback_flow`, a fixed airflow; `2` drive the declared `safe_flow`, the declared safe airflow — e.g. the permit-protecting rate for the low-DO direction [3(e), 19]); `fallback_flow`/`safe_flow` (`Float`). Which response a plant declares, and whether the code is operator-selectable, are the note's recorded customer-validation assumptions — declared data, never kind defaults; where operator-selectable is answered yes, the code rides `set_parameter` under its declared `ParameterRange`.
  Behavior: while `pv` reads `Good` the demand passes; a non-`Good` `pv` engages the declared response and asserts `fallback_active` for the engagement's duration; `out` carries the merged worst of `in`/`pv` so a held demand stays marked untrusted — the `flow-paced-ratio` quality rule; recovery resumes pass-through the first scan `pv` reads `Good`, and the transition is the alarmed event. The measurement architecture feeding `pv` stays composition — single probe, `failover-select` primary/backup [7, 12], averaging, or `median-voter` 2oo3 voting [19] — the note's recorded assumption this kind does not settle; `discrepancy`/`backup_active` feed the alarm set beside `fallback_active`.
  Checkpoint obligations: the held last-good demand is run state under decision 20's rule.
- **Alternatives considered:** An `on_bad` parameter added to `pid` (couples the general controller to measurement-loss policy — the fallback belongs on the demand path where the declared response is a contract element, and `pid` stays reusable where bad quality simply propagates); `interlock` tripped on the quality-derived condition (its `safe_value` is a fixed fallback — "hold last demand" needs state `interlock` does not carry, and the hold/fixed/safe code set is declared data a single safe value cannot enumerate); `manual-station` driven by quality wiring (its `mode` is an operator/mode input — conflating operator and failure switching loses the alarmed-transition distinction, decision 42's `override-select` reasoning); the voter's quality propagation alone (a `Bad` downstream demand is honest but holds no declared value — the note requires the fallback declared, not emergent).
- **Consequences:** One small kind carries the whole DO-loss contract — the declared response, the alarmed engagement, the honest downstream quality — and composes under every measurement architecture the note evidences. The response choice and its operator-selectability stay flagged for customer validation rather than defaulting; the kind ships its `dcs-build` spec under the drift guard.
- **Affected tickets:** #254 (this record); the M10 `demand-fallback` kind ticket — the kind plus its `dcs-build` spec under the drift guard (to be filed); the M10 aeration-train composition ticket (to be filed); the scripted acceptance-slice ticket exercising total DO loss and recovery (to be filed); requirements `WW-CTL-005` and `WW-OPS-003`; relies on #111, #211, #263, #219, #82 and decisions 2, 20, 42, 50; evidence `docs/research/aeration-do-control.md` (#241) sources [3(e), 7, 12, 19; the terminal-fallback absence the note records].

### 66. Feed-forward demand injection

- **Status:** Accepted — decided: a distinct additive form beside the landed `flow-paced-ratio`; the kind contract below is proposed and the M10 tickets (to be filed) land it where a plant declares the layer. Implemented basis it builds on: `flow-paced-ratio` landed (#263, decision 50) — `dose × flow × trim`, the `trim` scaling *multiplicatively*; `pid` provides the feedback trim loop [4 §6.3.2.1] (#11, #110); internal port-to-port links carry the cascade wiring (decision 14); `rate-limiter` bounds demand slew (#85); writable internal `In` points and `set_parameter` carry operator values and tuning (#30, #49, #82, decisions 14, 18, 20).
- **Problem:** The note's gap 5 — flow-paced or load-proportional air demand with the feedback portion trimming the feed-forward pacing [4 §6.3.2.1, 13, 15]. Recorded against what the dosing tickets landed: `flow-paced-ratio`'s `trim` is multiplicative — the analyzer output scales the paced demand — while aeration's documented form is additive, a computed air demand *summed* with a feedback correction [4 §6.3.2.1]. The DO loop's output is an airflow correction in demand units; feeding it onto `trim` would scale the feed-forward term by it, not add it — the contracts differ in the composition law, not just the port names — and no composition of `pid`s computes a sum (gap 5's reasoning).
- **Chosen approach (proposed):** A separate `feedforward-sum` kind rather than a second mode on `flow-paced-ratio`.
  Declared I/O (proposed): `ff` (`In`, `Float` — the computed feed-forward demand; where the pacing law is proportional — air per unit influent flow or load — a `flow-paced-ratio` with `trim` unwired computes it, the landed dosing kind reused as the ratio engine [4 §6.3.2.1, 15]), `trim` (`In`, `Float` — the feedback loop's additive correction, typically the DO `pid`'s `out`); `out` (`Out`, `Float` — the summed, bounded demand feeding the zone's demand path), `clamped` and `fallback_active` (`Out`, `Bool` — the decision-50 status vocabulary).
  Parameters (proposed — the decision-50 conventions): `trim_min`/`trim_max` (`Float` — the declared trim authority: the feedback portion trims, never owns, the demand [4 §6.3.2.1]); `min_demand`/`max_demand` (`Float` — the summed-demand bounds); `on_bad_ff`/`on_bad_trim` (`Int` codes — each input's declared untrusted response under the hold-versus-serve-other-term alternatives; the precise code set is the implementing ticket's documented contract — recorded as declared data, never defaulted).
  The ammonia-based supervisory variants compose beside this kind, not inside it: ABAC is an ammonia `pid` whose `out` is the DO loop's `sp` through the internal-link convention [9, 13, 17]; AvN and direct-ammonium forms are the same wiring with different declared loops [13, 14] — the note's set-point-wiring proposal, no new machinery. Whether feed-forward or an ammonia layer is in scope for the first client is the note's recorded assumption — the kind lands only where the declared plant demands the layer, and a DO-feedback-only zone wires nothing here.
- **Alternatives considered:** A second `trim` semantic on `flow-paced-ratio` selected by parameter (one kind carrying two composition laws hides which law a plant runs behind a code — the multiplicative and additive forms deserve distinct names in the registry and the descriptor); the additive trim left to `pid` output limits alone (no summation seam exists — `bool-gate` has no arithmetic semantics, the note's gap-5 reasoning); feed-forward inside `header-coordinator` (couples per-zone demand composition to bank-level coordination — the feed-forward layer serves one zone's demand); the DO `pid`'s output as the whole demand (that is the pure-feedback form — a different operating mode, not the feed-forward contract; both stay expressible and the primary-mode choice stays assumption-marked).
- **Consequences:** The additive-versus-multiplicative split is recorded honestly against the landed dosing kind; the feed-forward layer composes `flow-paced-ratio` as its pacing engine where the law is proportional and needs one small kind only for the bounded sum. The in-scope question stays flagged; the `dcs-build` spec obligation and the checkpoint conventions apply as to every kind.
- **Affected tickets:** #254 (this record); the M10 `feedforward-sum` kind ticket — the kind plus its `dcs-build` spec under the drift guard (to be filed); the M10 aeration-train composition ticket (to be filed); the scripted acceptance-slice ticket (to be filed); requirement `WW-CTL-005`; relies on #263, #110, #85, #82 and decisions 14, 18, 20, 50; evidence `docs/research/aeration-do-control.md` (#241) sources [4 §6.3.2.1, 9, 13, 14, 15, 17].

### 67. The blower device contract and mechanical-aerator scope

- **Status:** Accepted — decided: the `motor` + `analog-output` composition stands for the blower; mechanical aerators are recorded outside `WW-CTL-005`'s contract with a revisit condition. Implemented basis: `motor` echoes the run command and flags `fault` after `fault_ticks` of disagreeing feedback (#52); `analog-output` carries the Float capacity demand and `digital-input`/`digital-output` the status and command bits (#11); `valve` covers the vent/unloading valve (#52); `manual-station`/`override-select` cover operator takeover (#110); the `dcs-build` builder composes the bundle per machine (#112, decision 31); the decision-52 metering-pump question answered the same wrap-versus-compose question.
- **Problem:** The note's gaps 7 and 9. Whether a `blower` device kind wrapping `motor` + `analog-output` plus the protective-status family earns a contract over the plain composition — the metering-pump question decision 52 already answered for dosing — and whether mechanical surface/jet aerators [8 §3.4, 2(6)(b)] fall inside `WW-CTL-005`'s diffused-air scope. The actuation forms the evidence shows — VFD speed on PD machines, inlet throttling or VFD on multistage centrifugal, speed plus inlet guide vanes and variable diffuser vanes on single-stage centrifugal, and on/off staging [5 §4.3.1, 4 §6.5.1.3, 6 §2.2.1.1.11] — must each land on a declared seam.
- **Chosen approach (decided):** Stay at the composition: `motor` — run command out, run feedback in, the proven `fault` flag — plus `analog-output` carrying the machine's capacity demand (speed, guide-vane, or throttle position as the machine type dictates [5 §4.3.1]) through decision 64's `surge-guard`, plus the protective-status family as declared field inputs — per-bearing over-temperature, vibration, overload, oil system, the startup/shutdown override state, the PD pressure relief [6 §2.2.1.11.x] — feeding the `avail_i`/`fault_i` aggregation and the alarm set, with `blower-group`'s per-unit ports above (decision 63). Every actuation form the evidence shows is a Float demand or a Bool command the composition already types; which form the client's blowers present — and whether any machine couples the halves with a policy wiring cannot express, the condition that would reopen a `blower` kind — is the note's recorded customer-validation assumption.
  Mechanical aerators are outside the diffused-air contract — a recorded exclusion with a revisit condition, the decision-54 pattern. A surface or jet aerator is an on/off-or-speed machine that is also the mixer [8 §3.4, 2(6)(b)]: it composes as `motor` (plus `analog-output` where speed varies) with its mixing constraint carried as declared wiring — a declared minimum-on or availability pattern — not through the header coordinator, with which it shares no air header. Whether the first client runs mechanical aeration at all is the note's flagged assumption; if it does and the mixing interplay needs shared machinery — an aerator's minimum-on-time bound into a DO loop — this decision reopens rather than stretching the header contract.
- **Alternatives considered:** A `blower` kind bundling run, capacity, and protection status (the bundle is wiring — the coupling semantics live in `surge-guard` and `blower-group`, which stay separate kinds a run-only or unguarded machine can omit — decision 52's bundle reasoning); a device-level `blower` kind under the decision-29 registry (device kinds resolve drivers, not control semantics — the blower's channels are ordinary points and no driver behavior needs encapsulating); extending `motor` with a capacity demand port (rejected with decision 52 — discrete actuation stays uncoupled from the analog demand, and `motor` stays reusable alone); mechanical aerators inside the header contract (they share no header — including them would carry coordination machinery the form does not have, while their actual contract is the station's `motor` pattern).
- **Consequences:** Actuation needs no contract work — every evidenced form is expressible by including or omitting composition parts, and the demand path ends at a typed field point like every other output. The per-machine actuation form stays flagged machine data; the mechanical-aerator exclusion is recorded, not silent: `WW-CTL-005`'s acceptance covers the diffused-air train and a client naming mechanical aeration reopens the decision.
- **Affected tickets:** #254 (this record); the M10 aeration-train composition ticket wires the `motor` + `analog-output` + protective-status composition (to be filed); the scripted acceptance-slice ticket (to be filed); requirement `WW-CTL-005`; relies on #52, #11, #110, #112 and decisions 29, 31, 52, 63, 64; evidence `docs/research/aeration-do-control.md` (#241) sources [2, 4 §6.5.1.3, 5 §4.3.1, 6, 8 §3.4].

### 68. Energy and airflow accounting ownership

- **Status:** Accepted — decided: measured totalization lands in the control contract; derived energy metrics defer to `WW-REP-001` reporting with a recorded revisit. Implemented basis: `totalizer` integrates a Float `rate` with `reset`, `rollover`, and frozen-on-bad-quality semantics (#111); `counter` accumulates Bool edges (#85); per-point history and the durable journal supply the series reporting reads (#35, #163, decisions 17, 36); the `WW-REP-001` candidate entry carries the reporting scope.
- **Problem:** The note's gap 8. Aeration is the dominant energy load — 45–75% of facility electricity [10, 5 §4.1] — so its measurement is specified: mass airflow meters per zone downstream of the control valves, a header pressure transmitter, and per-blower discharge flow plus motor power for specific-power comparison (kW per 100 scfm logged at short intervals) [7 §3.4, 16].
