- **Alternatives considered:** Expiring announced records on a TTL (rejected alone — expiry answers the dead-hint leg but not the foreign-hint leg: a live interloper's fresh announce would keep redirecting the pulls, and a legitimate standby that goes quiet would lose its candidacy); preferring configured/successor sources over announced ones on the involuntary path (already the resolution order — the finding's rig had none configured, so ordering alone changes nothing); refusing to track at all without a configured source (rejected — strands every `--peer`-less pair the announced follow-peer contract exists to reconverge, decision 12); a per-scan re-probe with no suppression bound (rejected — a dead or hostile set would stall every scan cycle on a bounded pull burst; the `ANNOUNCED_VERIFY_RETRY` window keeps the re-probe bounded without making a recovered hint wait longer than one window); treating the announce itself as proof after the fact (rejected — the serving-side record cannot distinguish the puller's monitor port from any other same-source port, which is the reason the demote path verifies at all).
- **Consequences:** A foreign or dead `?peer=` announcer can no longer redirect an involuntarily demoted peer's tracking: the demoted run either adopts the hint the demote verify would have adopted — journaling the same `TrackingSourceAdopted` — or pulls nothing, reporting `Unsynchronized`/`Orphaned` rather than `Degraded` on a foreign document, and a dead hint's bounded-pass fallback plus bounded re-probe window replace the indefinite strand with an automatic reconvergence once a legitimate successor announces. Verify-pull traffic against an unproven endpoint is bounded at one pass — the demote verify's pull and the owner-resolution probe's — per `ANNOUNCED_VERIFY_RETRY` window per recorded set. `Monitor::tracking_source` remains the recorded-resolution read — diagnostics and the demote guard — while `verified_tracking_source` is the pull-time contract; both are public, so consumers reading the former for pull targeting must switch. Coverage is `dcs-monitor/tests/tracking.rs`: `an_involuntary_demote_verifies_the_announced_hints_before_tracking` (the reproduction — foreign probe plus dead hint recorded over the legitimate standby's announce, claim preempted, demoted peer adopts and journals the successor, foreign endpoint sees only bounded verify/orphan probes, pair reconverges) and `an_involuntary_demote_with_only_unproven_hints_pulls_nothing` (the refuse leg — one bounded verify pass, no adoption journaled, no tracking pull ever follows).
- **Affected tickets:** #873 (implemented — QA finding `involuntary-demote-unverified-announced-hint`); amends decision 12's announce contract (#514, #684, #807, #832 — the recorded hint is now never a pull target, only a verify candidate, on every demotion path), decision 15's demote resolution (#514 — the verify's scrutiny now covers the boundary-less involuntary demotion too), and decision 91's announced-set contract (#831 — the bounded set the demote verify probes is the same set the lazy pass probes); composes with #872's keyed-only announced-source contract (decision 12 — on an unkeyed run the recorded hints are inert outright, which is itself the refused-unproven leg of this fix); relies on decision 28's fencing path (#65, #508, #515 — `field_claim_lost` is the involuntary trigger this covers).

### 93. JSON Schema for the dynamics document

- **Status:** Implemented (#870): `dcs-plant-server --dynamics-schema` prints the draft 2020-12 schema `ProcessElement::json_schema` emits from `dcs-sim`'s `schema` module, and `crates/dcs-sim/tests/dynamics_schema.rs` plus `crates/dcs-plant/tests/dynamics_schema.rs` sweep the checked fixture corpus against it.
- **Problem:** The release contract records emitted JSON Schemas for the plant model (`dcs-model schema`, decision 40) and the served block-interface registry (`dcs-model interface-schema`, decision 82) so non-Rust consumers can validate documents against a pinned artifact. The dynamics document — the consumer-owned artifact the deployment manifest names — had no emitted schema: its grammar lived only in the serde types and `merge_dynamics`' per-element checks, so a consumer's CI could not screen a document before the merge ran, and the release record had no artifact to pin for it.
- **Chosen approach (implemented):** Decision 40's convention applied to the decision-24 document. The schema is emitted as data — a hand-maintained document in `dcs-sim`'s `schema` module, beside the element types it describes — not generated from the serde types, for the same recorded reason: it deliberately diverges from deserialization where the languages differ in power. It is *stricter* than serde where that serves engineering: `additionalProperties: false` inside every element's field object (serde silently ignores unknown keys, so a misspelled field would evaporate), `minProperties`/`maxProperties` pinning the externally tagged single-key shape, and `uniqueItems` on the element list (a sound approximation of the driven-point conflict rule — identical elements necessarily drive the same output, which the merge rejects as `ConflictingDriver`, so nothing valid is refused). It also writes down the numeric bounds `ChannelMap::validate` owns where the vocabulary can express them — `time_constant`, `damping_ratio`, and `delay` strictly positive, `amplitude` non-negative, `seed` bounded to `u64` — and the field shapes serde carries: `flow_sum`'s `bias` optional, `threshold`'s `initial` a `bool`. Emission rides the dynamics-check tooling: `dcs-plant-server --dynamics-schema` is a standalone mode needing no model — the schema describes the element vocabulary itself — printed in the canonical pretty serialization so the release record pins its sha256 like the other recorded schemas. The one grammar serves both merge surfaces: the sim-bus document carries register addresses in the same point-valued fields, so the same artifact covers `dcs-sim-bus-device --dynamics`.
- **Alternatives considered:** Emitting through `dcs-model` (a `dcs-sim` dependency in the serialization-contract crate — the same inversion decision 31 keeps out of `dcs-build`, coupling the model contract to testbench vocabulary the decision-24 boundary keeps separate); generating the schema from the serde types (rejected with decision 40 — derive cannot express the single-key shape's strictness or the numeric bounds, and would mirror serde's silent unknown-key acceptance); checking the schema inside `--check-dynamics` (the merge remains the operative validator — the schema is a first screen for external tooling, and a `jsonschema` runtime dependency in the server buys nothing the merge does not already reject with a named element and point); a `version` field or `const` stamp like the model schema's (the dynamics document deliberately carries no `version` — decision 24's bare declaration list — so nothing is stamped).
- **Consequences:** What the schema cannot express is recorded rather than implied, mirroring decision 40: point references binding to declared points of the kind each end requires (`UnknownPoint`, `ElementPointKind`, `ElementGateKind`, `ElementContactKind`), a `threshold`'s `on`/`off` being distinct (`NonPositiveBand` — comparing two fields is beyond the vocabulary), driven-point uniqueness across differing elements (`ConflictingDriver`), finiteness (a magnitude like `1e999` parses as a `number` the merge then rejects), and the lexical integer/float distinction. Those stay with deserialization and `ChannelMap::validate` at merge: a document passing the schema may still fail `--check-dynamics`, and the schema rejects some documents the merge would accept (unknown keys, out-of-range parameter values). The release contract records `dynamics.schema.json` beside the other recorded schemas; release records carry it from the first tag whose tooling emits it.
- **Affected tickets:** #870 (this record and implementation); decision 24 (the document and its boundary — #81); decision 40 (the schema convention — #159); decision 82's served-registry precedent (#394); requirements `WW-ENG-001` and `WW-ENG-003`.

### 94. An unclaimed field standing diverged on every peer recovers by restart-as-active — never by a promotion override

- **Status:** Decided (#915) — the recorded recovery for an unclaimed field holding un-commanded outputs while every tracked peer reports `diverged` is the operator-directed restart-as-active; no heal-on-promote rule and no plant-layer repair procedure enter the contract. No runtime changes. Implemented basis the decision rests on: an unclaimed field refuses every mutation — fail-closed since decision 85's #528 rule — while `ProbeWriter` reports the ownerless state as `field_claim: unclaimed` on the role report and `field_unclaimed` on the pair view (decision 90, #901); a quiesced peer stages its field `Out` image every scan and a same-tick staged-vs-field mismatch lands the named `StandbySync::Diverged` verdict, which `promote`/`self_promote` refuse `not_converged` and which clears only on a fully-read same-tick match (decision 26's #541 positive-evidence rule); and `Peer::activate` runs the conditional startup grant — `claim_writer_unless_held`, granted over a free or dead claim, refused only while a live different-owner incumbent's unyielded claim stands (decision 89's #797 semantics, extended by decision 91's yielded/controller marks).
- **Problem:** QA finding `diverged-field-wedge-no-controller-recovery` — the consuming defect is #730 — demonstrates the wedge: an interposer ran the protocol-legal sequence `claim_writer` preempt, one field `Out` write, `release_writer`, leaving the field unclaimed while holding an actuation value no run ever commanded — a state reachable non-maliciously as a maintenance write left unrestored. Every tracked peer then staged the same agreed image and compared it against the foreign field value: every peer reported `diverged` and every `POST /promote` answered `not_converged`. The wedge is self-sealing because the two verdicts guard different evidence: `diverged` clears only on a same-tick comparison matching a field a writer is writing, and `unclaimed` clears only on a claim landing — while the diverged verdict blocks the very promotion that would supply that writer. Decisions 86 and 89 record restart-as-active for a dead owner's *standing* claim and the conditional startup grant, but never named which actor recovers an *unclaimed* diverged field, whether promotion may heal when all tracked peers agree and only the field disagrees, or what the served surface tells the operator while the wedge stands.
- **Chosen approach (decided):** Restart-as-active is the recorded remedy — decision 86's recovery for the ownerless field, landing here on a claim that is simply free. The operator relaunches one controller instance as a fresh active on the same model: `Peer::activate`'s conditional startup grant takes the field because no claim stands — the refusal case the grant exists for, a live different-owner incumbent, is exactly what this wedge lacks — the restartee's first field-owning scans write its declared image over the un-commanded value, and the surviving peers' next same-tick staged-vs-field comparisons then match the new owner's writes, clearing each `diverged` verdict to `tracking` through the ordinary positive-evidence resync (journaled `DivergenceResolved`, decision 26). One relaunch heals the whole pair: the diverged peers need neither restart nor promotion — they keep pulling, keep staging, and reconverge in place on the new owner's stream. A reachable `--state-file`/`--journal-file` resumes the carried run at its last persisted cycle (decisions 35, 36); without persistence the restart is the honest cold start decision 86 already records — a deployment-coverage question, not a contract gap.

  The recovery is operator-directed, never automatic: `self_promote` cannot fire from a `diverged` verdict (the convergence proof stands voided), and no watchdog relaunches a peer, because the contract cannot distinguish an un-commanded field value from a commanded one — `unclaimed` proves only that no writer stands, not that the standing values are foreign (a shutdown's last writes read identically). Naming which side is truthful is the operator's judgment, exercised through the deliberate act of launching an authority, exactly as decision 86 framed it.

  The served surface directs the operator to that remedy: every peer's `GET /role` reports `field_claim: unclaimed`, which the pair view names `field_unclaimed` (decision 90's #901 surfacing); each peer's `sync` reports `diverged` carrying the mismatch evidence — point, staged value, field value — which itself names the exact field point holding the un-commanded actuation and what the run would write in its place; and the `not_converged` refusal a `promote` draws, carrying the `diverged` state, is the contract declining the wrong remedy — decision 90's "unclaimed: needs promote" direction applies to a *promotable* peer, which a standing `diverged` verdict denies being. The boundary with the sibling wedges is recorded rather than implied: where a peer reports `orphaned` — the unclaimed line without standing divergence evidence — decision 87's ordinary promote remains the lighter recovery, its conditional orphan claim (decision 91) granting on the free field; where every peer reports `diverged`, restart-as-active is the remedy this record names. Both cases recover through the same actor class — a peer whose convergence evidence the gate accepts, or a fresh authority the operator launches — and neither softens the gate.

  What reopens the question: the evidence decision 86 names, applied to the unclaimed wedge — a QA-lane scenario or customer case where no reachable `--state-file` existed, the restart's cold-start outputs moved the process somewhere the wedged peers' last-adopted state would not have, and an owner names recovery-to-carried-state an acceptance obligation; or a served staged-image comparison making "all tracked peers agree and only the field disagrees" provable to the gate itself — today staged images live inside each peer and are never exchanged, which is exactly the evidence a bounded heal-on-promote would need.
- **Alternatives considered:** A bounded heal-on-promote — permitting a `diverged` peer to promote while the field reports `unclaimed` (declined: it is the override decision 86 refused wearing a narrower bound — `unclaimed` attests no writer, not that the standing values are un-commanded, so the diverged peer still cannot tell "the field is foreign" from "I am skewed", and a skewed peer's promotion writes wrong outputs to the plant; `not_converged` never has an override); a documented plant-layer repair procedure — an operator or tool claims the field, restores the intended values, releases (declined as the recovery: no served surface publishes the run's intended image — the staged outputs live inside each diverged peer — so the restore writes another un-verified value, and even a perfect one leaves the `diverged` verdicts standing until a field-owning run's writes make the same-tick comparisons match; it can correct field content but cannot recover the pair); both; and leaving the wedge to an undifferentiated restart-the-pair procedure (rejected — a full restart discards the carried state a single restart-as-active preserves and lets the surviving peers reconverge in place, and the record exists to name the minimal actor).
- **Consequences:** The convergence gate's absoluteness extends to the ownerless case: `not_converged` keeps no override even when the field provably has no writer, because ownerless is not the same evidence as un-commanded — decisions 26, 28, and 86 stand unchanged. The wedge's remedy is one operator relaunch, and the diverged peers reconverge in place on the new owner's stream — no restart of the wedged peers, no forced promotion. While the wedge stands the field is frozen, not drifting — the fail-closed unclaimed rule refuses every mutation — so the hazard is a standing un-commanded actuation, bounded and fully named on the served surface by `field_unclaimed` plus the `diverged` evidence. Deployment keeps the same duty decision 86 assigned it: persistence coverage (`--state-file`/`--journal-file`) is the difference between a warm resume and the recorded cold start.
- **Affected tickets:** #915 (this record); #730 (the consuming defect — QA finding `diverged-field-wedge-no-controller-recovery`, severity high); consistent with and relying on decisions 26 (#541's positive-evidence clearing), 28 (#65's claim fencing and heartbeat gating), 85 (#501/#528's fail-closed unclaimed field), 86 (#562's restart-as-active recovery and the absolute `not_converged` gate), 87 and 91 (#778's orphaned verdict and #831's conditional orphan claim — the lighter escape where the verdict is `orphaned`, not `diverged`), 89 (#797's conditional startup grant), and 90 (#853/#857/#901's unclaimed-field signal); requirements `WW-LCM-001` and `WW-OPS-003`.
